\begin{abstract}Every move below was produced by relational analogy to named master games (human and/or top-engine -- the exact source is named per move). Each figure shows our position with Melanie's move (a) and the top retrieved analog positions (b--d) with the master move played there; the caption names the exact source and the piece-role correspondence under $\mu$ -- cross-type maps (a rook playing a queen's role) are called out, as no material/eval engine explains a move that way.\end{abstract}

\caption{\textbf{Move 8, Black: \texttt{Nh5}.} (a) Our position; Melanie plays \texttt{Nh5} (\texttt{f6h5}). (c) Retrieved from \texttt{lichess\_elite\_2024-09\_p0022\#1265} (ply 21) [structural distance $d$=58, QAP obj=43]: the master played \texttt{Ng6}; under $\mu$ our \emph{Nf3} plays the role of that game's \emph{Ne4}; cross-type roles: our \emph{n}f3 as their \emph{Q}d8, our \emph{N}b8 as their \emph{P}a7; transferred move \texttt{f6h5} \textbf{(endorses this move)}. (d) Retrieved from \texttt{lichess\_elite\_2024-09\_p0012\#2672} (ply 26) [structural distance $d$=59, QAP obj=52]: the master played \texttt{O-O-O}; under $\mu$ our \emph{Ke1} plays the role of that game's \emph{Ke1}; cross-type roles: our \emph{N}f6 as their \emph{R}h1, our \emph{B}c8 as their \emph{P}a2; transferred move \texttt{e8f8}. Synthesis: 2 retrieved analogs transferred to \texttt{Nh5} (summed QAP objective 87, the strongest correspondence among the candidate moves); Melanie committed it via the \texttt{search} selector.}
